The models I used included Claude, made by Anthropic. Chapter 6 describes a military targeting platform with Anthropic’s model inside it, and section 10.3 uses that arrangement as its central case for a gap in accountability law that no existing doctrine closes. So the book criticizes, by name, the company whose product helped assemble it.

Nothing asserted here is without a record behind it — the founding prompt, the persona assignments, the generation transcripts, the review passes, and the finishing campaign’s own plan, decisions, and revision history are all in the repository this book comes from, dated and unedited after the fact: https://github.com/iterabloom/ethical.superintelligence.

The argument in this book is mine. I chose what survived, revised what I kept, and I am responsible for what is wrong with it.

European Union (2024a). Regulation (EU) 2024/1689 (Artificial Intelligence Act), Article 101: Fines for Providers of General-Purpose AI Models. Official Journal of the European Union, 13 June 2024. AI Office may fine noncompliant general-purpose AI model providers up to 3 percent of global annual turnover or EUR 15 million, whichever is higher; applies to any provider whose model is offered in the EU regardless of where the company is based. European Commission AI Office. url: https://artificialintelligenceact.eu/article/101/ (visited on 08/25/2026).

— (2024b). Regulation (EU) 2024/1689 of the European Parliament and of the Council Laying Down Harmonised Rules on Artificial Intelligence (Artificial Intelligence Act). Official Journal of the European Union, 13 June 2024. Article 5 bans a small set of unacceptable-risk practices outright; a larger high-risk tier requires conformity assessment and ongoing monitoring; limited-risk systems face transparency duties; minimal-risk systems are largely unregulated. European Parliament and Council of the European Union. url: https://eur-lex.europa.eu/eli/reg/2024/1689/oj (visited on 08/25/2026).

Implementing template: European Commission AI Office, Explanatory Notice and Template for the Public Summary of Training Content for General-Purpose AI Models, published 24 July 2025; the Article 53(1)(d) obligation applies from 2 August 2025 for new models and from 2 August 2027 for models already on the market. url: https://eur-lex.europa.eu/eli/reg/2024/1689/oj/eng (visited on 08/25/2026).

Feathers, Todd (Apr. 2021). Proctorio Is Using Racist Algorithms to Detect Faces. Reports software researcher Lucy Satheesan’s finding that Proctorio’s facial-detection function shares OpenCV file names, and that the underlying OpenCV model, tested against nearly 11,000 FairFace faces, failed to detect Black faces 57% of the time against 33–40% for other groups. No peer-reviewed study confirms this Proctorio-specific figure; Yoder-Himes et al. (2022), sometimes cited for it, tested a different product. Vice (Motherboard). url: https://www.vice.com/en/article/proctorio-is-using-racist-algorithms-to-detect-faces/ (visited on 08/25/2026).

Documents the Instagram acquisition (April 2012, about $1B) and the WhatsApp acquisition (February 2014, about $19B, about $21.8B at closing), and argues they consolidated behavioral-data access. Judgment for Meta, 18 November 2025 (Boasberg, J., D.D.C.): no present monopoly power shown, the court finding TikTok and YouTube reasonable substitutes. url: https://www.ftc.gov/legal-library/browse/cases-proceedings/191-0134-facebook-inc-ftc-v-ftc-v-meta-platforms-inc (visited on 08/25/2026).

Feldstein, Steven (2019). The Global Expansion of AI Surveillance. Tech. rep. Carnegie Endowment for International Peace. url: https://carnegieendowment.org/research/2019/09/the-global-expansion-of-ai-surveillance (visited on 08/25/2026).

Fickinger, Arnaud et al. (2020). Multi-Principal Assistance Games. Generalizes cooperative inverse reinforcement learning to N principals with differing preferences, with the assisting agent acting to maximize the sum of principal payoffs. Cited for the generalization and for the social-choice impossibility results that apply to it; a companion paper, arXiv:2012.14536, presented at the NeurIPS 2020 Workshop on Cooperative AI, gives collegial mechanisms that circumvent Gibbard’s theorem. arXiv: 2007.09540 [cs.AI]. url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2007.09540 (visited on 09/01/2026).

Gordon, Mitchell L. et al. (2022). “Jury Learning: Integrating Dissenting Voices into Machine Learning Models”. In: Proceedings of the 2022 CHI Conference on Human Factors in Computing Systems (CHI ’22). Field evaluation: 18 moderators of online communities authored juries for a comment toxicity classification task. The resulting juries carried 2.9 times the representation of non-White jurors and 31.5 times that of non-binary jurors relative to the jury implied by the underlying toxicity dataset, and changed the model’s classifications on 14% of items. The 14% is a share of all items in the evaluation, not of contested or disagreed-upon ones. Verified 2026-08-30 against the paper’s own abstract and section 1. Stanford University. ACM. doi: 10.1145/3491102.3502004. url: https://doi.org/10.1145/3491102.3502004 (visited on 08/25/2026).

Hadfield-Menell, Dylan, Anca Dragan, et al. (2017). “The Off-Switch Game”. In: Proceedings of the Twenty-Sixth International Joint Conference on Artificial Intelligence (IJCAI-17). The incentive to preserve the off switch is shown to rise with the agent’s uncertainty about the objective and to fall as that uncertainty resolves or as the human is modeled as less rational; the paper states the resulting trade-off between the value a system generates and the strength of its incentive to accept oversight., pp. 220–227. doi: 10.24963/ijcai.2017/32. url: https://www.ijcai.org/proceedings/2017/0032.pdf (visited on 08/31/2026).

Haskins, Caroline (2020). ACLU Called Clearview AI’s Facial Recognition Accuracy Study ‘Absurd’. Published 10 February 2020. Reports Clearview AI’s claim of 100 percent accuracy across all racial and demographic groups, from a three-person panel the company assembled and its report called the “Independent Review Panel”. The panel searched 535 members of Congress plus Texas and California state legislators against Clearview’s own database of about 2.8 billion scraped images, judging whether the top two matches showed the same person; the ACLU method it claimed to be using had searched congressional photographs against 25,000 arrest mugshots. The ACLU’s response, through Jacob Snow of its Northern California affiliate: “The report is absurd on many levels and further demonstrates that Clearview simply does not understand the harms of its technology in law enforcement hands.” url: https://www.buzzfeednews.com/article/carolinehaskins1/clearview-ai-facial-recognition-accurate-aclu-absurd (visited on 08/25/2026).

Ethically Aligned Design: A Vision for Prioritizing Human Well-being with Autonomous and Intelligent Systems, First Edition. Tech. rep. Companion standards operationalizing its principles include IEEE 7000-2021 (Standard Model Process for Addressing Ethical Concerns during System Design) and IEEE 7010-2020 (Recommended Practice for Assessing the Impact of Autonomous and Intelligent Systems on Human Well-being). IEEE. url: https://standards.ieee.org/wp-content/uploads/import/documents/other/ead1e.pdf (visited on 08/25/2026).

IEEE Standards Association (2020). IEEE CertifAIEd AI Ethics Certification Program. Certification program built on four criteria — accountability, transparency, privacy and algorithmic bias — and open to policymakers, underwriters and HR staff as well as to developers. It grew out of the IEEE Ethics Certification Program for Autonomous and Intelligent Systems, created October 2018; IEEE announced the underlying consultation and quality-mark services in February 2020; and the City of Vienna became the first entity worldwide to earn the CertifAIEd mark in November 2021. url: https://forms1.ieee.org/CertifAIEd.html (visited on 08/25/2026).

In the Matter of the Search of an Apple iPhone Seized During the Execution of a Search Warrant on a Black Lexus IS300, California License Plate 35KGD203 (2016). Order Compelling Apple Inc. to Assist Agents in Search, No. ED No. CM 15-0451M (C.D. Cal. Feb. 16, 2016) (Pym, M.J.), issued under the All Writs Act, 28 U.S.C. § 1651. Magistrate Judge Sheri Pym’s order compelling Apple to write and sign software defeating an iPhone’s passcode protections. Apple moved to vacate; the case was mooted in March 2016 when the FBI accessed the phone via a third party, before any ruling. url: https://www.eff.org/cases/apple-challenges-fbi-all-writs-act-order (visited on 08/25/2026).

International Committee of the Red Cross (1977). Protocol Additional to the Geneva Conventions of 12 August 1949, and Relating to the Protection of Victims of International Armed Conflicts (Protocol I). 1125 UNTS 3, Arts. 48, 51(5)(b), 57. url: https://ihl-databases.icrc.org/en/ihl-treaties/api-1977 (visited on 08/25/2026).

International Development Research Centre (2024). Combining Forces for a New Phase of AI for Development: Africa and Beyond. AI4D Africa launched 2020 as a partnership of Canada’s IDRC and Sweden’s Sida (CAD20 million over five years); expanded in 2024 with the UK’s Foreign, Commonwealth and Development Office into South and Southeast Asia and the Pacific, with combined investment of more than CAD100 million. url: https://idrc-crdi.ca/en/news/combining-forces-new-phase-ai-development-africa-and-beyond (visited on 08/25/2026).

International Energy Agency (2025). Energy and AI. Tech. rep. Reports data-center electricity consumption at about 415 TWh in 2024, roughly 1.5% of global electricity use, growing at about 12% a year over the preceding five years — more than four times the growth rate of total electricity demand — and projects about 945 TWh by 2030 in its Base Case. Web-published without page numbers. Paris: IEA. url: https://www.iea.org/reports/energy-and-ai (visited on 08/25/2026).

International Organization for Standardization and International Electrotechnical Commission (2023). ISO/IEC 42001:2023 – Information Technology – Artificial Intelligence – Management System. Published December 2023. First international AI management-system standard; requires risk assessment, per-system impact assessment, and third-party supplier oversight, and is auditable by accredited certification bodies. ISO/IEC. url: https://www.iso.org/standard/42001 (visited on 08/25/2026).

International Telecommunication Union (2025). Facts and Figures 2025: Measuring Digital Development. Tech. rep. ITU’s press release announcing the report, 17 November 2025, states that “94 per cent of people in high-income countries use the Internet, in contrast to only 23 per cent in low-income countries,” and that “84 per cent of people in high-income countries hav[e] access to 5G, compared with only 4 per cent in low-income countries.” International Telecommunication Union. url: https://www.itu.int/itu-d/reports/statistics/facts-figures-2025/ (visited on 08/25/2026).

Irving, Geoffrey, Paul Christiano, and Dario Amodei (2018). AI Safety via Debate. OpenAI. arXiv: 1805.00899. url: https://arxiv.org/abs/1805.00899 (visited on 08/25/2026).

Jacobs, Julian and Jordan Canedy (May 2026). Did US Worker Retraining Reduce Participant Automation Exposure? arXiv:2605.03767. Analyzes over 23 million WIOA participation records, 2017–2023; finds classroom retraining rarely moves participants into less automation-exposed work and that employer-led apprenticeship outperforms it; wage gains are shown to be largely consistent with mean reversion rather than occupational change. url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.03767 (visited on 08/25/2026).

James, William (1890). The Principles of Psychology. Two chapters are cited. Chapter IV, “Habit”: habit as “the enormous fly-wheel of society,” and the warning that the young will “become mere walking bundles of habits.” Chapter X, “The Consciousness of Self”: the present thought appropriates the past ones, and personal identity rests on its finding in them “a warmth and intimacy” it recognizes as its own. The line “we are what we repeatedly do” is Will Durant’s compression of Aristotle, not James’s and not Aristotle’s own sentence. New York: Henry Holt. url: https://psychclassics.yorku.ca/James/Principles/ (visited on 08/29/2026).

Johnson, Khari (Jan. 2021). What Algorithm Auditing Startups Need to Succeed. Published 30 January 2021, and the only published account of what is inside the ORCAA audit document, which is otherwise behind a legal agreement. “It states that by the time ORCAA conducted the audit, HireVue had already decided to begin phasing out facial analysis.” The report “contains no analysis of AI system training data or code, but rather conversations about the kinds of harm HireVue’s AI could cause in conducting prebuilt assessments of early career job applicants across eight measurements of competency.” Records a stakeholder’s concern that facial analysis “may work differently for people wearing head or face coverings and disproportionately flag their application for human review,” and a researcher’s charge of ethics washing. VentureBeat. url: https://venturebeat.com/2021/01/30/what-algorithm-auditing-startups-need-to-succeed (visited on 08/30/2026).

Kahn, Jeremy (2021). HireVue Drops Facial Monitoring Amid A.I. Algorithm Audit. Reports the January 2021 release of an ORCAA algorithmic audit of HireVue’s assessments, which mostly gave the company good marks and recommended further investigation of accent bias and clearer communication to candidates. Notes that HireVue had announced the previous year that it had stopped scoring facial expressions, and gives the company’s stated reason: nonverbal data contributed roughly 0.25 percent to predictive power in most cases and about 4 percent for customer-facing roles. Also notes the 2019 EPIC complaint to the FTC alleging unfair and deceptive trade practices. Fortune. url: https://fortune.com/2021/01/19/hirevue-drops-facial-monitoring-amid-a-i-algorithm-audit/ (visited on 08/25/2026).

Karger, Ezra et al. (2023). Forecasting Existential Risks: Evidence from a Long-Run Forecasting Tournament. Tech. rep. FRI Working Paper No. 1, July 2023. Reports the 2022 Existential Risk Persuasion Tournament: 80 domain experts and 89 superforecasters gave median AI-caused-extinction-by-2100 probabilities of 3.9% and 0.38%. Forecasting Research Institute. url: https://forecastingresearch.org/pdf/existential-risk-persuasion-tournament.pdf (visited on 08/25/2026).

Kela (Social Insurance Institution of Finland) (2020). Results of the Basic Income Experiment: Small Employment Effects, Better Perceived Economic Security and Mental Wellbeing. Tech. rep. Final results of Finland’s 2017–2018 basic income experiment: 2,000 unemployed people received an unconditional monthly stipend of 560 euros; recipients reported higher life satisfaction and lower psychological strain, with small/complicated employment effects. Ministry of Social Affairs and Health, Finland. url: https://stm.fi/en/-/perustulokokeilun-tulokset-tyollisyysvaikutukset-vahaisia-toimeentulo-ja-psyykkinen-terveys-koettiin-paremmaksi (visited on 08/25/2026).

Kim, Hyunwoo et al. (2023). “FANToM: A Benchmark for Stress-testing Machine Theory of Mind in Interactions”. In: Proceedings of the 2023 Conference on Empirical Methods in Natural Language Processing (EMNLP). Benchmark built from conversations with information asymmetry, rather than passive narratives; finds systems that score well on passive belief-tracking degrade sharply in live, information-asymmetric exchanges. Singapore, pp. 14397–14413. url: https://aclanthology.org/2023.emnlp-main.890/ (visited on 08/25/2026).

Kitwood, Tom (1997). Dementia Reconsidered: The Person Comes First. Gives the definition of personhood the manuscript quotes: “It is a standing or status that is bestowed upon one human being, by others, in the context of relationship and social being.” The wording follows Kitwood’s own chapter 1, “On Being a Person,” as reproduced in the 2019 Open University Press volume Dementia Reconsidered, Revisited: The Person Still Comes First; circulating paraphrases sometimes drop “in the context of.” The page number given is for the 1997 first edition, on convergent secondary citations rather than a copy of that printing. Buckingham, UK: Open University Press.

The article that coined “militant democracy.” Part II appeared in the same volume, number 4, pages 638–658. Maurer, Roy (Feb. 2021). HireVue Discontinues Facial Analysis Screening. Dates the discontinuation to March 2020, ten months before the audit coverage. Menand, Lev (July 2026). The Federal Reserve Exception to the Slaughter Rule. Just Security. July 7, 2026. Also posted to SSRN. “Policy as history” is Menand’s, in: “We are left with the distinct impression that Cook is ‘policy as history’ and what is motivating Chief Justice Roberts and Justice Kavanaugh is a concern with the consequences of extending the Slaughter Rule to the Federal Reserve.” It is an impression he arrives at, not a label he applies, which is why the text says he reads the exception as it rather than that he calls it so. The essay’s “stable doctrinal equilibrium” was also quoted here until 2026-08-30, as a carve-out with nothing stable under it, and was cut as a misattribution on three counts: the sentence is “Where does this leave us? Probably not with a stable doctrinal equilibrium that could rival the Humphrey’s Executor Era (1935–2020) or the Marbury Era before it (1803–1899)”, which is hedged, is about the whole post-Slaughter landscape rather than the Fed carve-out, and is comparative against two named eras rather than absolute. Verified against the essay 2026-08-30. url: https://www.justsecurity.org/145707/slaughter-categorical-rule-cook-exception/ (visited on 08/29/2026).

— (2023). GPT-4 System Card. Tech. rep. OpenAI. url: https://cdn.openai.com/papers/gpt-4-system-card.pdf (visited on 08/25/2026).

Human raters preferred the instruction-tuned 1.3-billion-parameter model to the 175-billion-parameter model it was built from. arXiv: 2203.02155. url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2203.02155 (visited on 08/28/2026).

Palley, Thomas I. (2015). “Money, Fiscal Policy, and Interest Rates: A Critique of Modern Monetary Theory”. In: Review of Political Economy 27.1. Argues that sustained large deficits pay a growing interest-income stream to bondholders, itself a fiscal injection with distributional consequences. (Palgrave Macmillan, 2019)., pp. 1–23. doi: 10.1080/09538259.2014.957466. (Visited on 08/25/2026).

In: Proceedings of the 40th International Conference on Machine Learning (ICML). 134 text environments with roughly 572,000 annotated scenario-action pairs, scored on reward and on ethical violations; finds reward-only optimization drives ends-justify-the-means behavior. arXiv: 2304.03279. url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2304.03279 (visited on 08/25/2026).

Thornley, Elliott et al. (2025). Towards Shutdownable Agents via Stochastic Choice. Trains agents to prefer between same-length trajectories and to lack a preference between different-length ones, so that usefulness and neutrality about shutdown timing come apart. The authors report that neutrality degrades with imperfect reward normalization, that generalization beyond deterministic gridworlds is untested, and that a sufficiently misaligned agent may resist shutdown regardless. arXiv: 2407.00805. url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.00805 (visited on 08/31/2026).

Trump v. Cook (2026). No. 25A312, 609 U.S. ____ (June 29, 2026). Interim docket; the disposition is “Application for stay denied.” 5–4. Roberts, C.J., for the Court, joined by Sotomayor, Kagan, Kavanaugh and Jackson, JJ.; Kavanaugh and Jackson, JJ., concurring; Thomas, J., Alito, J. (joined by Gorsuch, J.), and Barrett, J., dissenting. Upholds the Federal Reserve Act’s for-cause removal protection, on the Fed’s descent from the First and Second Banks of the United States, its budget free of congressional control and its privately owned regional banks, and holds that a Governor is owed notice and a hearing before removal. The constitutional holding is real and is what Thomas, J., dissents from, but the case is disposed of narrowly, on the ground that Cook did not receive the process the statute required: the Court states that it has “not addressed the facts, as they have yet to be found or analyzed under the relevant legal standards,” and Kavanaugh, J., concurring, opens that “[t]oday’s interim ruling does not decide whether the President may lawfully remove Governor Cook for cause.” The dispute is live — on 7 August 2026 the President notified Cook that he was again considering her removal, giving her 21 days to respond and citing this opinion as his authority for the procedure. Verified 2026-08-30. url: https://www.law.cornell.edu/supremecourt/text/25A312 (visited on 08/29/2026).

Trump v. Slaughter (2026). No. 25-332, 609 U.S. ____ (June 29, 2026). 6–3. Roberts, C.J., for the Court; Gorsuch, J., concurring; Sotomayor, J., dissenting, joined by Kagan and Jackson, JJ. Holds the Federal Trade Commission Act’s for-cause removal protection unconstitutional and overrules Humphrey’s Executor v. United States (1935): “If anything more is left of Humphrey’s, we overrule it.” url: https://www.law.cornell.edu/supremecourt/text/25-332 (visited on 08/29/2026).

Trump v. Wilcox (2025). No. 24A966, 605 U.S. ____ (May 22, 2025) (per curiam, on application for stay). Stay of orders reinstating a member of the National Labor Relations Board and a member of the Merit Systems Protection Board; Kagan, J., dissenting, joined by Sotomayor and Jackson, JJ. Describes the Federal Reserve as “a uniquely structured, quasi-private entity that follows in the distinct historical tradition of the First and Second Banks of the United States.” url: https://www.supremecourt.gov/opinions/24pdf/24a966_1b8e.pdf (visited on 08/29/2026).

Issued 12 May 2022 alongside a companion EEOC technical assistance document, EEOC-NVTA-2022-2. Uesato, Jonathan et al. (2022). Solving Math Word Problems with Process- and Outcome-Based Feedback. Outcome supervision matched process supervision on final-answer accuracy while using less labeled data; among correct final answers the reasoning-error rate was 14.0 percent against 3.4 percent. arXiv: 2211.14275. url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2211.14275 (visited on 08/28/2026).

UK AI Security Institute (2025). We Are Now the AI Security Institute. Established November 2023 as the AI Safety Institute; rebranded AI Security Institute on 14 February 2025. An independent pre-deployment evaluator of frontier models with no statutory power to compel testing, block release, or intervene once a system is causing harm. AI Security Institute (UK Department for Science, Innovation and Technology). url: https://www.aisi.gov.uk/category/governance (visited on 08/25/2026).

UK Department for Science, Innovation and Technology (Mar. 2023). A Pro-Innovation Approach to AI Regulation. Tech. rep. Published 29 March 2023; directs existing sector regulators to apply five cross-cutting principles (safety/security/robustness, transparency and explainability, fairness, accountability and governance, contestability and redress) rather than creating a new AI regulator or statute. UK Government. url: https://www.gov.uk/government/publications/ai-regulation-a-pro-innovation-approach/white-paper (visited on 08/25/2026).

Ullman, Tomer (2023). Large Language Models Fail on Trivial Alterations to Theory-of-Mind Tasks. arXiv: 2302.08399. url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2302.08399 (visited on 08/25/2026).

National Conference of Commissioners on Uniform State Laws, Uniform Probate Code, Official Text with Comments (as amended through 2018), p. 263. url: https://www.uniformlaws.org/viewdocument/final-act-with-comments-114?CommunityKey=a539920d-c477-44b8-84fe-b0d7b1a4cca8&tab=librarydocuments (visited on 08/25/2026).

United Nations General Assembly (Dec. 1948). Universal Declaration of Human Rights. UNGA Resolution 217 A (III). Adopted in Paris, 48 in favor, none against, 8 abstentions (Byelorussian SSR, Czechoslovakia, Poland, Saudi Arabia, South Africa, Ukrainian SSR, USSR, Yugoslavia), 2 absent (Honduras, Yemen). url: https://www.un.org/en/about-us/universal-declaration-of-human-rights (visited on 08/25/2026).

— (2023). Resolution 78/241: Lethal Autonomous Weapons Systems. A/RES/78/241, adopted 22 December 2023. Adopted 22 December 2023 by 152 votes to 4 with 11 abstentions, on the report of the First Committee (A/78/409). A follow-on resolution, A/RES/79/62 of 2 December 2024, passed 166 to 3 with 15 abstentions. The Campaign to Stop Killer Robots, founded April 2013, is the advocacy coalition the manuscript describes alongside this resolution and is not itself the cited document. United Nations. url: https://docs.un.org/en/a/res/78/241 (visited on 08/25/2026).

United States District Court for the Northern District of California (2025). Bartz et al. v. Anthropic PBC, No. 3:24-cv-05417, Order on Motion for Summary Judgment on Fair Use (N.D. Cal. June 23, 2025). Order by Senior District Judge William Alsup. Held that training Claude on lawfully purchased/scanned books was “exceedingly transformative” fair use, while retaining pirated copies in a “central library” was not fair use; the piracy claims later settled for $1.5 billion, reported as the largest copyright recovery on record. url: https://www.courtlistener.com/docket/69058235/bartz-v-anthropic-pbc/ (visited on 08/25/2026).

University of Chicago Data Science Institute (2026). Using AI and Data Science to Reliably Detect Internet Censorship in Real Time. University of Chicago Data Science Institute news. Describes a $1 million DARPA-funded project led by Prof. Nick Feamster (University of Chicago) with Princeton University and SRI International, using AI/statistical methods to distinguish deliberate censorship from ordinary network failure and produce a real-time map of state interference with internet traffic. url: https://datascience.uchicago.edu/news/using-ai-and-data-science-to-reliably-detect-internet-censorship-in-real-time/ (visited on 08/25/2026).

University of Helsinki (2020). Elements of AI Online Course to Expand Throughout the European Union. Elements of AI, created by the University of Helsinki and the Finnish company Reaktor, was translated into all official EU languages, an effort tied to Finland’s 2019 Presidency of the Council of the EU. url: https://www.helsinki.fi/en/news/teaching/elements-ai-online-course-expand-throughout-european-union (visited on 08/25/2026).

Vaswani, Ashish et al. (2017). “Attention Is All You Need”. In: Advances in Neural Information Processing Systems 30 (NeurIPS 2017), pp. 5998–6008. url: https://arxiv.org/abs/1706.03762 (visited on 08/25/2026).

Vygotsky, Lev S. (1978). Mind in Society: The Development of Higher Psychological Processes. Ed. by Michael Cole et al. Cambridge, MA: Harvard University Press. (Visited on 08/25/2026).

Waldron, Jeremy (2006). “The Core of the Case Against Judicial Review”. In: Yale Law Journal 115. Argues that judicial review is democratically illegitimate and that there is no reason to suppose rights are better protected by it than by a legislature. The case is explicitly conditional on a society having working democratic institutions and a citizenry that takes rights seriously. Cited as the standing objection an entrenched floor has to answer., pp. 1346–1406.

Watson, Gary (1975). “Free Agency”. In: The Journal of Philosophy 72.8. Separates a valuational system from a motivational system, so that what an agent values and what an agent is moved by can come apart., pp. 205–220. doi: 10.2307/2024703. (Visited on 08/31/2026).

Wei, Jason et al. (2022). Chain-of-Thought Prompting Elicits Reasoning in Large Language Models. Prompting a model to produce intermediate reasoning steps sharply improves multi-step problem solving. arXiv: 2201.11903. url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2201.11903 (visited on 08/28/2026).

Announces the worldwide extension, effective January 21, 2019, of the five-chat forwarding limit WhatsApp first imposed for users in India in July 2018, after viral rumors on the platform were tied to a string of mob killings. WhatsApp Blog. url: https://blog.whatsapp.com/more-changes-to-forwarding (visited on 08/25/2026).

Wisconsin Supreme Court (2016). State v. Loomis. 881 N.W.2d 749 (Wis. 2016), 2016 WI 68. Decided July 13, 2016; upheld consideration of a COMPAS risk-assessment score at sentencing over a due-process challenge, without requiring disclosure of the proprietary algorithm. url: https://www.wicourts.gov/sc/opinion/DisplayDocument.pdf?content=pdf&seqNo=171690 (visited on 08/25/2026).

Wray, L. Randall et al. (Apr. 2018). Public Service Employment: A Path to Full Employment. Tech. rep. Simulates a Public Service Employment program paying $15/hour plus benefits, employing an estimated 11–16 million people, at a net budgetary cost of 0.8–2 percent of GDP, with GDP rising and private-sector employment increasing by 3–4 million. Levy Economics Institute of Bard College. url: https://www.levyinstitute.org/publications/public-service-employment-a-path-to-full-employment/ (visited on 08/25/2026).

Zhao, Jieyu et al. (2017). “Men Also Like Shopping: Reducing Gender Bias Amplification using Corpus-level Constraints”. In: Proceedings of the 2017 Conference on Empirical Methods in Natural Language Processing (EMNLP). Zhu, Ligeng, Zhijian Liu, and Song Han (2019). “Deep Leakage from Gradients”. In: Advances in Neural Information Processing Systems 32 (NeurIPS 2019). Shows that private training data can be recovered from shared gradients — pixel-wise for images, token-wise for text — overturning the assumption that gradients are safe to share. url: https://arxiv.org/abs/1906.08935 (visited on 08/29/2026).

Zhuang, Simon and Dylan Hadfield-Menell (2020). “Consequences of Misaligned AI”. In: Advances in Neural Information Processing Systems 33. Gives necessary and sufficient conditions under which indefinitely optimizing an incomplete proxy objective — one with support on a subset of the attributes the principal values — drives overall utility arbitrarily low, in a resource-constrained world. Cited as the formal form of the aestheticized metric. arXiv: 2102.03896 [cs.AI]. url: https://arxiv.org/abs/2102.03896 (visited on 09/01/2026).
